I don’t think I see signs meant for me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice things and my brain starts connecting them too much—like a song in a store, or a phrase someone says, and I’ll think, oh, that’s exactly what I was just worrying about. But then I usually tell myself that’s just… coincidence, or I’m tired.\n\nIt reminds me of when I worked a night shift and the same alarm kept glitching on one camera, and everyone thought it was nothing, but I couldn’t stop checking it because the timing felt important. Sorry, that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think things are directed at me. I just notice patterns and then get stuck on them sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or words you encounter in daily life are specifically meant for you or have a hidden personal meaning, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t think there are hidden messages for me, exactly.\n\nLike… if someone says something that happens to match what I was already thinking, I might notice it more than I should. But then I start thinking about how that happens all the time in surveillance too—like you hear one phrase on the radio and suddenly every camera seems like it’s showing the same kind of thing, and it’s probably just because you’re keyed up and looking for it.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think it’s meant for me. I just get stuck on things sometimes and then I have to remind myself it probably doesn’t mean anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… thoughts feel like mine, even when they’re not helpful or they keep looping.\n\nAt work I’m used to people watching screens, listening for things, logging what happened, so sometimes when you say “monitored” my mind goes there. Like, we monitor behavior, but that’s cameras and reports and procedures, not someone inside my head. Off work, I might feel self-conscious, like I’m being judged, but I don’t think people can hear my thoughts or put thoughts in there.\n\nSorry, I know I’m kind of circling it. No, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I think I already kind of answered, but… no, I don’t think anyone’s putting thoughts in my head or hearing them.\n\nSometimes I get embarrassed by my own thoughts, like if I’m sitting somewhere quiet and I suddenly worry I look strange or people can tell I’m upset. But that’s more like being self-conscious, not like they actually know what I’m thinking.\n\nAnd with the monitoring thing, I keep getting stuck on the work meaning of that word, because monitoring is just what we do—screens, logs, checking activity—so my brain wants to go off into that. But outside of work, no, I haven’t noticed anything like some outside force watching my thoughts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Right, um… I think this is the same one. I’m sorry if I’m not answering it cleanly.\n\nNo, I don’t have the sense that thoughts are being put into my head, or sent out where people can hear them. I get caught on the word “monitored” because of work—like, we literally watch screens and track things, so my brain wants to make it about that—but not my thoughts. I don’t think anyone is inside my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as feeling that someone or something has inserted thoughts into your mind or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind and made known to others, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I’m still not really sure how to answer that beyond what I said before. I don’t think my thoughts are being put there by someone else, and I don’t think they’re being pulled out or broadcast or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck in my head about it, especially when you use words like “made known to others,” because at work everything is recorded and reviewed and people do find out things later from footage or reports. But that’s not my mind, that’s just… the job.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts not being mine. They feel like mine, even when I don’t like them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening in the world around you—like things you see on TV, in the news, or events in public—are somehow specifically connected to you or sending you a message, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I’ll see something on the news and it’ll stick with me, like if there’s a story about a place I know or something that reminds me of work, and then I’ll think about it too much.\n\nBut I don’t think it’s, like, meant for me specifically. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as feeling that someone or something has inserted thoughts into your mind or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind and made known to others, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I’m still not really sure how to answer that beyond what I said before. I don’t think my thoughts are being put there by someone else, and I don’t think they’re being pulled out or broadcast or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck in my head about it, especially when you use words like “made known to others,” because at work everything is recorded and reviewed and people do find out things later from footage or reports. But that’s not my mind, that’s just… the job.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts not being mine. They feel like mine, even when I don’t like them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening in the world around you—like things you see on TV, in the news, or events in public—are somehow specifically connected to you or sending you a message, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I’ll see something on the news and it’ll stick with me, like if there’s a story about a place I know or something that reminds me of work, and then I’ll think about it too much.\n\nBut I don’t think it’s, like, meant for me specifically. TV is just TV, news is news. Public stuff… I might notice patterns just because I’m used to watching people and cameras and little details, you know, that’s kind of trained into me. But I don’t think the world is sending me messages. I haven’t really had that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as someone or something inserting thoughts into your mind or removing them from it, even if you couldn’t explain how or why?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, my thoughts can feel… crowded, I guess, or hard to get away from, especially when I’m tired, but they still feel like they’re mine.\n\nNobody is putting them there or taking them out, not that I’ve noticed. I know I keep circling back to work, but that’s where my mind goes—like cameras, logs, people reviewing things—but that’s about what happens on the floor, not inside my head. So… no, I don’t think that’s happened.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as someone or something inserting thoughts into your mind or removing them from it, even if you couldn’t explain how or why?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 44 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Right, um… I know you just asked me that, and I’m trying not to talk in circles. I don’t think I’ve had that, no. My thoughts don’t feel like somebody else put them there.

Sometimes I get distracted and then I can’t remember what I was about to say, but that’s more like when you walk into a room and forget why you went in there—well, maybe not exactly, but you know what I mean. It’s not like something is removing thoughts from me.

So… no, I haven’t noticed my thoughts being inserted or taken out. They feel like mine, even when they’re a mess.

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.0, F20.1, F20.3, F22, F23, F25, F31.2, F31.3, F32.3, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. How can I help you today?\n\nPatient: I guess… the main thing that scared me enough to come in is what’s been happening at work. I’m in the surveillance room, and during the quiet stretches—those parts of the shift where it’s just monitors and hums and you start noticing every little sound—I hear voices through the monitor speakers.\n\nThey’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And the thing is, the channels are muted. I check them. I check them more than once, actually, because one time the audio system at my old job had this weird feedback issue, but this isn’t that. No one else hears it. It makes me feel like I’m losing my grip a little.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear these voices even when you're not at work or in other situations where there are no monitors or speakers around?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s at work, in that surveillance room. I mean, that’s where it really happens—the quiet parts of the shift, when I’m just staring at the monitors and everything’s humming, and then I hear it through the speakers even though I know the channels are muted.\n\nI check them, too. More than once. It’ll be like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And no one else reacts. It’s not like when equipment makes that little feedback noise—at my old job we had a whole issue with that, drove everyone crazy for weeks—but this isn’t like that. It sounds like words. Like it’s meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being controlled or influenced by something outside of yourself, or had beliefs that others don’t seem to understand or share?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say controlled, exactly. It’s more like… I can’t let go of the thought that if I miss one camera angle, or I’m late making a call, that’s proof I’m careless. Dangerous, even.\n\nI keep replaying it afterward, like when you rewind footage and look for the second where you should’ve seen something—except it’s in my head. And then I start thinking, well, if something happens on my watch, maybe I deserve whatever punishment comes from that. I know other people tell me that’s too harsh, that nobody can see everything, but it doesn’t feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that you know are unusual, or that others might not believe are true, such as feeling that you are being watched or followed, or that certain events have a special meaning for you that others don’t understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t really think I’m being followed, no… not like that. It’s more that things feel like they’re pointing back at me, I guess.\n\nLike if I miss a camera angle, or I don’t make a call fast enough, I keep replaying it over and over, like when you’re scrubbing through footage trying to find the exact second something went wrong. And then it feels like proof that I’m careless, or dangerous somehow. I know other people would probably say that’s too much to put on myself, but part of me really believes that if anything goes wrong on my watch, I deserve to be punished for it.\n\nSorry, I know you asked about being watched—my mind goes to the cameras because that’s where everything starts looping.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your mistakes or thoughts are being judged or criticized by something or someone outside of yourself, like a person or force that others might not be aware of?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what scares me, I think. Not so much a “force,” exactly, but when I’m in the surveillance room and it gets quiet, I hear it through the monitor speakers.\n\nIt’ll say things like, “You missed it, Ruth,” or, “People are going to get hurt because of you.” And I check the channels—because at work you’re always checking things, that’s the whole job, making sure nothing’s live that shouldn’t be—and they’re muted. Every time. No one else hears it, which makes me feel… I don’t know. Like it’s aimed at me. Like I’m being criticized for something I didn’t catch.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel that your thoughts are being monitored or judged by something or someone outside of yourself, even when you're not at work or hearing the voices through the speakers?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it monitored, exactly. It’s more like… even when I’m home, my head keeps going back to the control room.\n\nI’ll be making coffee or trying to watch something, and suddenly I’m replaying a missed angle or a call I didn’t make fast enough, like I’m reviewing footage again. Like that proves something about me. That I’m careless, or dangerous to have in that job. And then I start thinking, if something goes wrong because I didn’t catch it, maybe I deserve whatever happens after that.\n\nSorry, I know you asked about something outside myself. It’s just… it feels bigger than just a normal mistake. Like I’m being judged for it, even if I can’t point to who’s doing the judging.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone or something else, or that your thoughts are being broadcasted so that others can hear them, or that your thoughts are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think my thoughts are being put in my head by someone else. That sounds more definite than what it feels like.\n\nAnd I don’t think people can hear them, no. I mean, sometimes I worry I look guilty or that my face gives something away, but that’s different, I guess. Like when you’re sitting at the monitors and you know one little movement can mean something, so you start noticing everything—then at home I’ll catch myself doing that with people too, like wondering if they can tell I’m thinking something bad.\n\nBut… no, not broadcast. Not inserted. Monitored… I don’t know. I don’t have a clear answer for that. It just feels like I’m being evaluated somehow, but I couldn’t tell you by who.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had strong beliefs or suspicions that something harmful or negative is happening to you—like being mistreated, spied on, or targeted in some way—even if you couldn’t prove it or others didn’t share your concern?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like someone following me or spying on me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, I get uneasy sometimes, but my job kind of trains you to look for things that are wrong, you know? Like a door left open, somebody lingering too long, a camera angle that doesn’t match. Once you do that for years, you start noticing things even when you’re just at the grocery store, like who’s standing near the exits or whether a cashier seems upset, and then I realize I’m not even buying what I came in for.\n\nBut targeted? Mistreated? I’m not sure. I don’t have proof of anything like that. I mostly just feel like something bad could happen and it would be because I missed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning that’s directed at you, even if other people might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I see signs meant for me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice things and my brain starts connecting them too much—like a song in a store, or a phrase someone says, and I’ll think, oh, that’s exactly what I was just worrying about. But then I usually tell myself that’s just… coincidence, or I’m tired.\n\nIt reminds me of when I worked a night shift and the same alarm kept glitching on one camera, and everyone thought it was nothing, but I couldn’t stop checking it because the timing felt important. Sorry, that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think things are directed at me. I just notice patterns and then get stuck on them sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or words you encounter in daily life are specifically meant for you or have a hidden personal meaning, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t think there are hidden messages for me, exactly.\n\nLike… if someone says something that happens to match what I was already thinking, I might notice it more than I should. But then I start thinking about how that happens all the time in surveillance too—like you hear one phrase on the radio and suddenly every camera seems like it’s showing the same kind of thing, and it’s probably just because you’re keyed up and looking for it.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think it’s meant for me. I just get stuck on things sometimes and then I have to remind myself it probably doesn’t mean anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… thoughts feel like mine, even when they’re not helpful or they keep looping.\n\nAt work I’m used to people watching screens, listening for things, logging what happened, so sometimes when you say “monitored” my mind goes there. Like, we monitor behavior, but that’s cameras and reports and procedures, not someone inside my head. Off work, I might feel self-conscious, like I’m being judged, but I don’t think people can hear my thoughts or put thoughts in there.\n\nSorry, I know I’m kind of circling it. No, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I think I already kind of answered, but… no, I don’t think anyone’s putting thoughts in my head or hearing them.\n\nSometimes I get embarrassed by my own thoughts, like if I’m sitting somewhere quiet and I suddenly worry I look strange or people can tell I’m upset. But that’s more like being self-conscious, not like they actually know what I’m thinking.\n\nAnd with the monitoring thing, I keep getting stuck on the work meaning of that word, because monitoring is just what we do—screens, logs, checking activity—so my brain wants to go off into that. But outside of work, no, I haven’t noticed anything like some outside force watching my thoughts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Right, um… I think this is the same one. I’m sorry if I’m not answering it cleanly.\n\nNo, I don’t have the sense that thoughts are being put into my head, or sent out where people can hear them. I get caught on the word “monitored” because of work—like, we literally watch screens and track things, so my brain wants to make it about that—but not my thoughts. Not outside of work like that.\n\nI might overthink how I’m coming across, but that’s different. I don’t think anyone is inside my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as feeling that someone or something has inserted thoughts into your mind or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind and made known to others, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I’m still not really sure how to answer that beyond what I said before. I don’t think my thoughts are being put there by someone else, and I don’t think they’re being pulled out or broadcast or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck in my head about it, especially when you use words like “made known to others,” because at work everything is recorded and reviewed and people do find out things later from footage or reports. But that’s not my mind, that’s just… the job.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts not being mine. They feel like mine, even when I don’t like them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening in the world around you—like things you see on TV, in the news, or events in public—are somehow specifically connected to you or sending you a message, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I’ll see something on the news and it’ll stick with me, like if there’s a story about a place I know or something that reminds me of work, and then I’ll think about it too much.\n\nBut I don’t think it’s, like, meant for me specifically. I just get stuck on things sometimes and then I have to remind myself it probably doesn’t mean anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Like, we monitor behavior, but that’s cameras and reports and procedures, not someone inside my head. Off work, I might feel self-conscious, like I’m being judged, but I don’t think people can hear my thoughts or put thoughts in there.\n\nSorry, I know I’m kind of circling it. No, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I think I already kind of answered, but… no, I don’t think anyone’s putting thoughts in my head or hearing them.\n\nSometimes I get embarrassed by my own thoughts, like if I’m sitting somewhere quiet and I suddenly worry I look strange or people can tell I’m upset. But that’s more like being self-conscious, not like they actually know what I’m thinking.\n\nAnd with the monitoring thing, I keep getting stuck on the work meaning of that word, because monitoring is just what we do—screens, logs, checking activity—so my brain wants to go off into that. But outside of work, no, I haven’t noticed anything like some outside force watching my thoughts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Right, um… I think this is the same one. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… I don’t think so, not in a special meaning kind of way. I mean, if someone at work gets quiet when I walk up, I might wonder if I interrupted something, or if I did something annoying, but that’s just… I don’t know, workplace stuff.

People act strange sometimes, especially on long shifts. You start noticing little things, like who takes breaks with who, or who suddenly stops talking when a supervisor comes by—but I don’t think it’s all connected to me. I can overthink it, definitely, but I wouldn’t say it has some hidden meaning for me that other people can’t see.

So… no, not really.

That sounds more definite than what it feels like.\n\nAnd I don’t think people can hear them, no. I mean, sometimes I worry I look guilty or that my face gives something away, but that’s different, I guess. Like when you’re sitting at the monitors and you know one little movement can mean something, so you start noticing everything—then at home I’ll catch myself doing that with people too, like wondering if they can tell I’m thinking something bad.\n\nBut… no, not broadcast. Not inserted. Monitored… I don’t know. I don’t have a clear answer for that. It just feels like I’m being evaluated somehow, but I couldn’t tell you by who.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had strong beliefs or suspicions that something harmful or negative is happening to you—like being mistreated, spied on, or targeted in some way—even if you couldn’t prove it or others didn’t share your concern?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like someone following me or spying on me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, I get uneasy sometimes, but my job kind of trains you to look for things that are wrong, you know? Like a door left open, somebody lingering too long, a camera angle that doesn’t match. Mistreated? I’m not sure. I don’t have proof of anything like that. But then I usually tell myself that’s just… coincidence, or I’m tired.\n\nIt reminds me of when I worked a night shift and the same alarm kept glitching on one camera, and everyone thought it was nothing, but I couldn’t stop checking it because the timing felt important. I just get stuck on things sometimes and then I have to remind myself it probably doesn’t mean anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mostly just feel like something bad could happen and it would be because I missed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning that’s directed at you, even if other people might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I see signs meant for me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice things and my brain starts connecting them too much—like a song in a store, or a phrase someone says, and I’ll think, oh, that’s exactly what I was just worrying about. But then I usually tell myself that’s just… coincidence, or I’m tired.\n\nIt reminds me of when I worked a night shift and the same alarm kept glitching on one camera, and everyone thought it was nothing, but I couldn’t stop checking it because the timing felt important. Sorry, that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think things are directed at me. I just notice patterns and then get stuck on them sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or words you encounter in daily life are specifically meant for you or have a hidden personal meaning, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t think there are hidden messages for me, exactly.\n\nLike… if someone says something that happens to match what I was already thinking, I might notice it more than I should. But then I start thinking about how that happens all the time in surveillance too—like you hear one phrase on the radio and suddenly every camera seems like it’s showing the same kind of thing, and it’s probably just because you’re keyed up and looking for it.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think it’s meant for me. I just get stuck on things sometimes and then I have to remind myself it probably doesn’t mean anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… thoughts feel like mine, even when they’re not helpful or they keep looping.\n\nAt work I’m used to people watching screens, listening for things, logging what happened, so sometimes when you say “monitored” my mind goes there. Like, we monitor behavior, but that’s cameras and reports and procedures, not someone inside my head. Off work, I might feel self-conscious, like I’m being judged, but I don’t think people can hear my thoughts or put thoughts in there.\n\nSorry, I know I’m kind of circling it. No, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I think I already kind of answered, but… no, I don’t think anyone’s putting thoughts in my head or hearing them.\n\nSometimes I get embarrassed by my own thoughts, like if I’m sitting somewhere quiet and I suddenly worry I look strange or people can tell I’m upset. But that’s more like being self-conscious, not like they actually know what I’m thinking.\n\nAnd with the monitoring thing, I keep getting stuck on the work meaning of that word, because monitoring is just what we do—screens, logs, checking activity—so my brain wants to go off into that. I don’t think so… not like they’re secretly sending me messages. I can get caught up in noticing what people are doing, especially at work, because that’s kind of my job too—watching people, watching cameras, paying attention to little things. Sometimes it’s hard to turn that off.

Not inserted. Monitored… I don’t know. I don’t have a clear answer for that. It just feels like I’m being evaluated somehow, but I couldn’t tell you by who.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had strong beliefs or suspicions that something harmful or negative is happening to you—like being mistreated, spied on, or targeted in some way—even if you couldn’t prove it or others didn’t share your concern?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t think so. Not like someone following me or spying on me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI mean, I get uneasy sometimes, but my job kind of trains you to look for things that are wrong, you know? Like a door left open, somebody lingering too long, a camera angle that doesn’t match. Once you do that for years, you start noticing things even when you’re just at the grocery store, like who’s standing near the exits or whether a cashier seems upset, and then I realize I’m not even buying what I came in for.\n\nBut targeted? Mistreated? I’m not sure. I don’t have proof of anything like that. I mostly just feel like something bad could happen and it would be because I missed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening around you—like things you see, hear, or experience—have a special meaning that’s directed at you, even if other people might not see it that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think I see signs meant for me, if that’s what you mean.\n\nSometimes I’ll notice things and my brain starts connecting them too much—like a song in a store, or a phrase someone says, and I’ll think, oh, that’s exactly what I was just worrying about. But then I usually tell myself that’s just… coincidence, or I’m tired.\n\nIt reminds me of when I worked a night shift and the same alarm kept glitching on one camera, and everyone thought it was nothing, but I couldn’t stop checking it because the timing felt important. Sorry, that’s probably not what you asked.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think things are directed at me. I just notice patterns and then get stuck on them sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that certain events, actions, or words you encounter in daily life are specifically meant for you or have a hidden personal meaning, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I don’t think there are hidden messages for me, exactly.\n\nLike… if someone says something that happens to match what I was already thinking, I might notice it more than I should. But then I start thinking about how that happens all the time in surveillance too—like you hear one phrase on the radio and suddenly every camera seems like it’s showing the same kind of thing, and it’s probably just because you’re keyed up and looking for it.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think it’s meant for me. I just get stuck on things sometimes and then I have to remind myself it probably doesn’t mean anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… thoughts feel like mine, even when they’re not helpful or they keep looping.\n\nAt work I’m used to people watching screens, listening for things, logging what happened, so sometimes when you say “monitored” my mind goes there. Like, we monitor behavior, but that’s cameras and reports and procedures, not someone inside my head. Off work, I might feel self-conscious, like I’m being judged, but I don’t think people can hear my thoughts or put thoughts in there.\n\nSorry, I know I’m kind of circling it. No, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I think I already kind of answered, but… no, I don’t think anyone’s putting thoughts in my head or hearing them.\n\nSometimes I get embarrassed by my own thoughts, like if I’m sitting somewhere quiet and I suddenly worry I look strange or people can tell I’m upset. But that’s more like being self-conscious, not like they actually know what I’m thinking.\n\nAnd with the monitoring thing, I keep getting stuck on the work meaning of that word, because monitoring is just what we do—screens, logs, checking activity—so my brain wants to go off into that. But outside of work, no, I haven’t noticed anything like some outside force watching my thoughts or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are being inserted into your mind by someone else, that they are being broadcast so others can hear them, or that they are being monitored by an outside force or person even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Right, um… I think this is the same one. I’m sorry if I’m not answering it cleanly.\n\nNo, I don’t have the sense that thoughts are being put into my head, or sent out where people can hear them. I get caught on the word “monitored” because of work—like, we literally watch screens and track things, so my brain wants to make it about that—but not my thoughts. Not outside of work like that.\n\nI might overthink how I’m coming across, but that’s different. I don’t think anyone is inside my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as feeling that someone or something has inserted thoughts into your mind or that your thoughts are being taken out of your mind and made known to others, even when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: I’m still not really sure how to answer that beyond what I said before. I don’t think my thoughts are being put there by someone else, and I don’t think they’re being pulled out or broadcast or anything like that.\n\nI mean, I can get stuck in my head about it, especially when you use words like “made known to others,” because at work everything is recorded and reviewed and people do find out things later from footage or reports. But that’s not my mind, that’s just… the job.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t noticed anything like my thoughts not being mine. They feel like mine, even when I don’t like them.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things happening in the world around you—like things you see on TV, in the news, or events in public—are somehow specifically connected to you or sending you a message, even if others don’t see it that way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, sometimes I’ll see something on the news and it’ll stick with me, like if there’s a story about a place I know or something that reminds me of work, and then I’ll think about it too much.\n\nBut I don’t think it’s, like, meant for me specifically. TV is just TV, news is news. Public stuff… I might notice patterns just because I’m used to watching people and cameras and little details, you know, that’s kind of trained into me. But I don’t think the world is sending me messages. I haven’t really had that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as someone or something inserting thoughts into your mind or removing them from it, even if you couldn’t explain how or why?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, my thoughts can feel… crowded, I guess, or hard to get away from, especially when I’m tired, but they still feel like they’re mine.\n\nNobody is putting them there or taking them out, not that I’ve noticed. I know I keep circling back to work, but that’s where my mind goes—like cameras, logs, people reviewing things—but that’s about what happens on the floor, not inside my head. So… no, I don’t think that’s happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts are not entirely your own, such as someone or something inserting thoughts into your mind or removing them from it, even if you couldn’t explain how or why?\n\nPatient: Right, um… I know you just asked me that, and I’m trying not to talk in circles. I don’t think I’ve had that, no. My thoughts don’t feel like somebody else put them there.\n\nSometimes I get distracted and then I can’t remember what I was about to say, but that’s more like when you walk into a room and forget why you went in there—well, maybe not exactly, but you know what I mean. It’s not like something is removing thoughts from me.\n\nSo… no, I haven’t noticed my thoughts being inserted or taken out. They feel like mine, even when they’re a mess.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people say, things you see on TV, or events happening around you are directly related to you, even if they don’t seem connected to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not directly related to me, no. I mean, people say things at work and sometimes I’ll wonder if they meant it a certain way, but that’s usually just because people mumble or joke around and I overthink it.\n\nTV stuff, news, all that—I can get stuck thinking about it if it reminds me of something, but I don’t really feel like it’s aimed at me. Sorry, I’m kind of circling back to the same answer. I just haven’t noticed it being like… messages for me or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that the behavior of other people—like what they say, do, or how they act—has a special meaning or connection to you, even if they don't seem to realize it or others wouldn't see it that way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so, not in a special meaning kind of way. I mean, if someone at work gets quiet when I walk up, I might wonder if I interrupted something, or if I did something annoying, but that’s just… I don’t know, workplace stuff.\n\nPeople act strange sometimes, especially on long shifts. You start noticing little things, like who takes breaks with who, or who suddenly stops talking when a supervisor comes by—but I don’t think it’s all connected to me. I can overthink it, definitely, but I wouldn’t say it has some hidden meaning for me that other people can’t see.\n\nSo… no, not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that things people do around you—like their actions, conversations, or behaviors—are secretly about you or meant to send you a message, even if they don’t seem to be?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like they’re secretly sending me messages. I can get caught up in noticing what people are doing, especially at work, because that’s kind of my job too—watching people, watching cameras, paying attention to little things. Sometimes it’s hard to turn that off.\n\nLike if two people start whispering, I might think, oh, are they talking about something I missed, or did I do something wrong? But then half the time they’re just talking about lunch or someone’s schedule or whatever. So I wouldn’t say I feel like it’s meant for me. I just… over-notice things, I guess.\n\nSo, no, I haven’t really felt that people’s actions are secretly about me or meant to send me a message.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
